## 0. One-page summary

**For a customer (template clone or `npx logicloom` install)**
- Updates become a short list of named improvements, each with one plain sentence on what it does for you and one yes/no, instead of a list of changed file paths.
- A file you never changed takes the new version; a file you did change is adapted around your edits by a read-only agent, and you see a plain description of the result before anything lands.
- An improvement that closes a safety hole cannot be skipped, and a script proves it actually took effect in your copy before the update is kept.
- Nothing of yours is deleted or overwritten without your yes; a retired plugin you customised stays, with an explanation of what replaced it.
- The whole update is one commit on your branch with a checkpoint tag; "undo the update" reverses exactly that commit and verifies every file came back.
- You get one approval prompt from the governance gate per update (the moment it lands), not one per git command.
- Your product code, `features/`, `specs/` and `.brain/` are never touched; a cheap tree-hash check proves it on every update.
- The first time, you run your existing `/update-framework` once; it brings the new engine in, and from then on you run `/update-loom`. `/update-framework` keeps working as a pointer.

**For the maintainer**
- Every change that reaches customers ships with a note: purpose, which files, a done-when list, and (for safety or protected files) an executable check.
- A release gate refuses to promote while any shipped change has no note; the note linter runs in CI and at `/promote`.
- Deleting a plugin directory needs a note that says so (`removes:`) — this replaces `check-release`.
- 7.0.0 should be a thin "bootstrap" release: the engine, notes, agents, skill and command, little else, so it passes cleanly through the old updater.

**Retired**: the v7 scanner (`release-migrations.sh`, its library and registry, `record-update-writes.sh`), the ownership and scanner test suites, `MIGRATION_GUIDE.md`, the old proposal extractor and skill. About 18,500 lines go; about 8,000 come in, half of them tests.

**Cost**: four build phases; the first slice (engine + two fixture notes + the end-to-end test) proves the model before anything else is written. Estimated 8,000 new lines; see section 7.

**What could go wrong**
- A note's done-when is too loose and an adaptation passes that reads wrong (silent; mitigated by the reviewer agent and by always showing the plain-words result).
- The engine runs git inside one script where the hooks cannot see it; we accept that for the throwaway worktree and tag, and make the one irreversible-feeling step (landing) a visible, gated command.
- Customers on a broken sync baseline (v6.3.1/v6.4.0/v6.4.1/v6.5.0 tag clones) cannot run the old updater at all and need a documented repair or a re-install first — this design does not paper over it.
- Legacy `sdd-*` directories cannot be told apart from a customer's own by content (no reference copy exists in public history), so they are kept by default and only the wiring is checked.

---

## 1. The model, as ported

### 1.1 What stays from Launchhouse, in one paragraph

The engine is a git three-way file engine with a notes layer (A §1.0): every tracked path in the union of base, HEAD and upstream trees (`LH:update.sh:931-941`) is classified by comparing three blobs against a real base commit; untouched files take the new version, both-changed files merge or are held; only held files reach the adapter. Notes supply purpose, the plain-words question, the safety flag and an executable outcome check. No ownership is ever inferred from content (`LH:update.sh:546-677`). LogicLoom's base commit is `.sdd-sync-ref`, which both channels hold (`payload-manifest.txt:199`; B §6.1) and whose tree is local after the fetch-only pull into `refs/loom-upstream/main` (`v6.6.2:extract-proposals.sh:92-102`).

### 1.2 Where things live (ships to both channels unless marked)

| Artefact | Path | Why there |
|---|---|---|
| Engine | `plugins/loom-maintenance/scripts/update-loom.sh` | floor-scanned (`test_bash32_floor.sh:177-195`), payload `include: plugins` (`payload-manifest.txt:122`), receipt records the dir (kori receipt lists `plugins/loom-maintenance/`) |
| JSON tools | `plugins/loom-maintenance/scripts/json-valid.sh`, `json-flat.sh` | verbatim from LH (POSIX sh + awk, `LH:json-flat.sh:1-35`) |
| Linter, checks runner | `plugins/loom-maintenance/scripts/updates-lint.sh`, `updates-checks.sh` | same tree, same floor |
| Notes + checks | `plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/<release>/<id>.md`, `<id>.check.sh` | always upstream-owned (1.6); `<release>` is the version, e.g. `7.0.0` |
| Skill | `plugins/loom-maintenance/skills/update-loom/SKILL.md` | Principle XVI; `constitutional-check.sh:698-730` warns on unmarked `.claude/skills/*` |
| Command | `plugins/loom-maintenance/commands/update-loom.md` + bridge stub `.claude/commands/update-loom.md` | via `sync-plugin-commands.sh sync` |
| Agents | `.claude/agents/update-adapter.md`, `.claude/agents/update-reviewer.md` | project agents keep `tools:` (constraint H); payload `include: .claude/agents` (`:175`) |
| Retired names | `.logic-loom/config/retired-plugins.txt` | flat list for the six CI consumers (B §0.9); python validator can read it |
| Channel map | `.logic-loom/config/update-channels.conf` | adopt-excluded prefixes + renames, generated from `payload-manifest.txt` at promote (1.4) |
| Coverage gate | `.logic-loom/scripts/bash/update-coverage.sh` | MAINTAINER-ONLY, stripped (section 5) |
| State | `$(git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir)/loom/update/` | per clone, never committed (`LH:update.sh:62-66` pattern) |

Not `.claude/updates/` or `.claude/scripts/`: unscanned by the floor and absent from the adopt payload and the receipt's recorded dirs (B §0.8).

### 1.3 Note schema

Launchhouse's fields kept verbatim (`LH:.claude/updates/README.md`, Frontmatter): `id title purpose touches adds requires safety done-when check`. Renamed and added:

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| `project-data` (was `founder-data`) | `true` when the note proposes a change to a **fork-authored** file LogicLoom never shipped. Allowlist, shipped in the linter: `.logic-loom/memory/amendments.md`, `.logic-loom/config/project.conf`, `environments.conf`, `gate-policy.conf` posture lines, `.claude/rules/*`. A `project-data` note: is never `safety: true`; has no upstream blob, so the engine can only adapt, never take; its check may test only the specific defect pattern, never conformance to LogicLoom wording; declining it is final (not re-offered). Body section `## What this note may claim` replaces `## Migration`. |
| `removes` (new) | Paths that must be absent after the note applies. The linter asserts each is absent in the upstream tree (the mirror of the `touches` exists-rule, `LH:updates-lint.sh:234`). Needed because a deletion note's targets do not exist upstream. |
| `channels` (new, default `both`) | `template` or `adopted` on a note whose `touches` include a path that exists in only one channel (root `CLAUDE.md`, `tests/`, `.github/` are adopter-owned in an adopted repo: `payload-manifest.txt:365,424,458`). The engine skips the note's rows in the other channel with a one-line reason. |

**Forbidden-touch set** (constraint F; linter refuses, engine refuses, gate proves): `web/`, `apps/`, `features/`, `specs/`, `.brain/` except `.brain/README.md`, `node_modules/`, and in an adopted repo everything the channel map marks adopter-owned. Launchhouse's five-layer `growth-engine/` protection (A §3, last paragraph) ports with this list substituted: excluded from the path universe, `path_ignored`, apply-loop skip, `--adapt-save` refusal, linter rule, and the **tree-sha equality gate** (`git rev-parse HEAD:web` etc. equal between worktree and live, `LH:update.sh:1363-1368`), plus undo's refusal of a commit touching them (`LH:update.sh:2497-2502`).

**Protected paths** (constraint A): a note whose `touches` include anything in `loom_protected_path_tokens` (`governance-verdicts.sh:891-912`: `.claude/hooks`, `.claude/settings.json`, `gate-policy.conf`, `governance.conf`, `constitution.md`, `loom-governance/hooks`, …) must name an executable `check` (not `none`). `safety` stays the author's call. Linter rule.

### 1.4 Channels, one engine (constraint D)

Detection: `.logicloom-adopt-receipt.json` with `"schema": "logicloom/adopt-receipt@1"` → adopted; else template (HEAD `update-framework.md:35`). Both channels: base = `.sdd-sync-ref`; upstream = `refs/loom-upstream/main` after the ad-hoc fetch (no remote — `framework-upstream.conf:8-12`; Launchhouse's `git remote add upstream`, `LH:update.sh:72-79`, is dropped: `git.remote.write = ask`, `gate-policy.conf:160`). "Which notes apply" (1.5) is identical in both channels because it reads two trees that both have.

Differences, all data in `update-channels.conf`, generated at promote from `payload-manifest.txt`:
- adopter-owned paths (every `exclude:` row): rows for them are skipped in an adopted repo, never proposed, never checked;
- renames (`rename: AGENTS.md :: .logic-loom/AGENTS.md`, `:316`): the engine re-addresses the note's row and the three-way sides;
- merge-channel files (`:330,337`: `.claude/settings.json`, `.gitignore`): always held in an adopted repo; the adapter's `base` side for `settings.json` is the recorded insertion `.claude/.logicloom-adopt-settings.json` (kori has it), not upstream's file, since the adopter's file never equalled upstream's.

Shipping the exclude list as data is new work; today it exists only in the unshipped `packaging/` manifest (B §6.1).

### 1.5 Which notes apply — presence plus outcome

Launchhouse: a note applies iff present in upstream's tree and absent from the base commit's tree, or standing in `held.tsv` (`LH:update.sh:802-820`). That rule alone breaks LogicLoom's first run after bootstrap (B §0.6: after the old updater, `.sdd-sync-ref` already points at the tree that contains the notes) and silently drops declined notes (A §3.2). Ported rule:

> A note applies iff it is in the upstream tree **and** any of: (a) absent from the base tree; (b) its `check` exits non-zero against the live tree; (c) its id is in `held.tsv` or `declined.tsv`.

(b) is the outcome test doing the work the registry's inference used to do. (c) with `declined.tsv` (`id<TAB>base<TAB>date`, same shape as `held.tsv`, `LH:update.sh:2353-2374`) makes the re-offer a one-line "you declined this before — still skip?" (Launchhouse's own recommendation, `LH:docs/purpose-based-updates.md` §11 Q2). A `project-data` note is exempt from (b) and (c): declined is final. Notes and checks are read from `refs/loom-upstream/main` into state at plan time, never from the working tree (`LH:update.sh:780-787`; B §5.4 point 2).

### 1.6 Notes tree is upstream-owned

Any path under `plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/` that differs from upstream is `take`/`delete`, never held or kept (`LH:update.sh:560-576`), and is exempt from `.gitignore` suppression (`LH:update.sh:381-386`). A customer cannot edit a safety note away.

### 1.7 Check contract

Runs as `sh <file>` with the repo root as cwd and `REPO_ROOT` exported; POSIX sh + awk only; exit 0 = purpose holds; on failure the **last** line is `hint=<one plain sentence>` (`LH:README.md`, The check file). JSON is read by content via `json-flat.sh`, never by line shape (lesson A §2.2 #8). Hook commands in LogicLoom's `settings.json` are `bash <relpath>` (`.claude/settings.json:2-89`; kori-beta identical, plus one adopter hook `bash scripts/hooks/guard-prod-db.sh`), so the shipped `hooks-resolve` helper extracts the first `*.sh` token, requires it to exist, be readable and pass `sh -n`/`bash -n`, and **never executes it** (`LH:update.sh:1487-1524`). It gates only on paths under harness-owned trees (`.claude/hooks/`, `plugins/loom-*/`, `.logic-loom/`) and warns on the rest — a missing adopter hook is theirs, not a reason to stop their update (template-conformance rule, A §2.2 #7).

Two check kinds, by note: **safety** — must pass in every copy; missing check, `check: none`, malformed `safety:` or any non-zero exit is FAIL (`LH:updates-checks.sh:165-214`). **Non-safety** — in a customer copy a failure is `WARN` and the run stays green; in the template repo it is FAIL (`LH:updates-checks.sh:93-105, 204-206`). Copy mode detector: template = `tests/lib/tree-provenance.sh` says "maintainer" (its five marker files all present, `:28-40`); anything else is a customer copy. The hold-back pass (`LH:update.sh:1855-2072`) re-runs each non-safety check in the worktree and sets aside only the failing improvement.

### 1.8 Linter

Port of `LH:updates-lint.sh:155-236` plus: `removes` absent upstream; `project-data` allowlist; protected-path ⇒ `check` required; `channels` ∈ {both, template, adopted}; forbidden-touch set; no `ID_MARKERS` (`leak-guard.sh` list: `brian@kelleysd.com`, `kelleysd.com/`, `ioun-ai`, `/workspaces/`) and no `~/.claude` reference in any note body (B §0.7). Runs in `plugin-tests.yml`, in `/promote` step 2, and first inside `updates-checks.sh`.

### 1.9 `requires` chains, declined notes

`requires` is schema-only in Launchhouse (A §1.13). Here: `--plan` orders notes topologically; a note whose `requires` names a declined or held note is shown as **blocked** with the blocker's title and is not applied; a safety note may not `requires` a non-safety note (linter) so the half-applied case in LH risk 3 cannot arise. Declined: 1.5.

---

## 2. The `/update-loom` flow

Actors: **engine** (`update-loom.sh`, deterministic, POSIX sh); **skill** (main agent, the only thing that talks to the user and the only thing that types git); **update-adapter** and **update-reviewer** (project agents, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob`, no Bash — the engine pre-writes every diff and log they need into state, so the reviewer's `git show/diff/log` allowance in `LH:update-reviewer.md:18-20` is unnecessary and its prose-only enforcement is gone); **user**.

### 2.1 Git operations mapped to `gate-policy.conf` (constraint A)

The hooks are a string gate on the command the agent types (`git-safety-gate.sh:12-16`); git run inside `sh update-loom.sh` is invisible to them (B §5.3). The design therefore puts every git mutation into one of two buckets:

| Step | Who runs it | Where it happens | Gate verdict | Why acceptable |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `git fetch <url> +refs/heads/main:refs/loom-upstream/main` | engine (`--plan`) | local ref only | would be `git.fetch = silent` (`gate-policy.conf:208`) | same as today's extractor |
| `git tag loom/pre-update-<ts>` | engine (`--stage`) | local tag | `git.tag.write = silent` (`:178`) | the old skill does the same (`v6.6.2:SKILL.md` Step 6) |
| `git worktree add -b loom-update/<ts> <state>/wt HEAD`, `git add`, `git commit`, `--amend` (hold-back), `worktree remove`, `branch -D` | engine (`--stage`, `--abandon`, `--finish`) | inside `.git/loom/update/`; live tree untouched | `worktree.write`/`branch.write` are `ask` (`:151,156`) **when typed**; here invisible | reversibility 0: nothing in the working tree changes; the worktree and branch are throwaway; the refusal set (`is_clean`, HEAD and upstream unchanged since plan, `LH:update.sh:1631-1653`) runs first. Decision 9.2 offers the visible alternative |
| **`git merge --ff-only loom-update/<ts>`** | **skill, typed visibly** | live branch | **`git.merge = ask` (`:103`)** | the one prompt per update; it is the moment the live tree changes, it carries a commit touching protected paths, and it is where Principle VI's approval belongs. The command text names no protected token, so `protect-governance-files.sh`'s Bash branch stays quiet; one prompt, not two |
| post-landing smoke, auto-undo via `git revert --no-edit` | engine (`--finish`) | live branch | `git.revert = silent` (`:196`) | additive by construction; verified by content (`LH:update.sh:2439-2448`) |
| `--undo` → `git revert --no-edit <recorded commit>` | engine, after the user says "undo" | live branch | silent | same |
| `git reset --hard` | **never** | — | `git.reset.hard = ask` (`:120`) | Launchhouse's undo-verification failure path (`LH:update.sh:2522`) is replaced: `git revert --abort` where a revert is in progress, otherwise print `result=reverted-failed`, `pre_update_tag=` and stop; a person decides |
| `git push` | **never** | — | FLOOR | Launchhouse's skill pushes (`LH:SKILL.md` step 10.1); LogicLoom does not. `/git-push` is the user's path |

Subagents: neither agent has Bash, so no agent in this flow can run the engine or git. Landing requires a typed git command, which `subagent-git-guard.sh` denies for any subagent in every posture (`gate-policy.conf:56-58`). A subagent outside this flow could at most run `--stage` (staging a worktree inside `.git`), never land it. UNVERIFIED: no environment marker tells a child process it was launched by a subagent (B §5.3), so the engine itself cannot refuse on that basis.

Protected-path writes (`.claude/settings.json`, hooks): the engine writes them only in the worktree; they reach the live tree through the gated merge. The adapter never writes anything (constraint H); the skill saves its returned body with `--adapt-save`, which validates path membership, forbidden set, symlinks, and JSON validity with upstream's own validator saved at plan time (`LH:update.sh:968-1081`, `:871-876`).

### 2.2 Step by step (both channels; channel differences in brackets)

0. **Preflight** (skill → engine `--status`): channel, `clean` (ignores untracked files outside harness paths — kori has an untracked `.claude/launch.json`, B §2), `in_progress`, upstream URL from `framework-upstream.conf`. **Bootstrap check** (section 3): if the local engine differs from upstream's copy, run upstream's copy from `<gitdir>/loom/bootstrap/` for this one update (`LH:SKILL.md` "First update on an older copy"). Dirty tree → stop and ask the user to commit; the skill does not commit for them.
1. **Base** (`--detect-base`): `recorded <sha>` from `.sdd-sync-ref` if it is a commit reachable from `refs/loom-upstream/main`; else the known-bad remap (`v6.6.2:extract-proposals.sh:127-138`, kept); else `closest <sha> <n>` by fewest differing harness-owned files across first-parent public history (`LH:update.sh:300-330`), confirmed by the user; else `none` → stop with the repair text from `KNOWN_ISSUES.md:55-112`. `--set-base` commits `.sdd-sync-ref` alone (lesson A §2.2 #15; `git.commit = silent`).
2. **Plan** (`--plan`, 10-minute Bash timeout, idempotent): fetch; copy notes+checks from upstream into state; classify every path; apply channel map; compute note applicability (1.5); write `plan.tsv`, `notes.tsv` (with a `blocked`/`declined-before` column), `adapt.tsv` with base/theirs/mine sides, `diffs/`, and `reviewer/<path>.log` (pre-materialised `git log` for the reviewer). If every row is `keep`/unchanged and no note applies: "You are up to date."
3. **Ask per improvement** (skill, AskUserQuestion, batched): for **every** applying note, not only those with held rows (fixes A §3.1). Safety: *Apply* / *Tell me more*. Non-safety: *Take this update* / *Keep mine as it is*. Declined-before: one line, *Still skip* recommended. `project-data`: *Adapt my file* / *Leave it*. Blocked: shown, not asked. Paths no note covers (the bootstrap span only, section 5 keeps it empty afterwards): one group, "other upstream changes", with the reviewer's bullets.
4. **Adapt** (skill dispatches `update-adapter` per held row whose note was approved, concurrently): brief = path, the three sides from state, the approved notes' purpose/done-when/body (`LH:update-adapter.md` rules carry over: never drop a base hook, never loosen a permission, safety fix wins over the customisation that is the hole, preserve shape and CRLF, JSON must parse). Skill writes the body to `state/adapt/out/<path>` and calls `--adapt-save`. `cannot-adapt`/refusal: safety → `take-theirs` (customer's copy preserved at the tag and in `state/mine/`); otherwise `hold`.
5. **Second read** (`update-reviewer`, read-only): waterfall items 1, 3, 7, 8 of `LH:update-reviewer.md` (dependency grep, settings customisation, merged-clean sense read, adapted-vs-purpose) plus the new item from LH §11 Q3: *is each prose done-when checkable and does it match the purpose*. May only make a row more cautious.
6. **Show and one yes** (skill): what is new; kept as you made it; one sentence per adapted file; held items; blocked items; then one clear yes. Developer audience: paths and a diff are offered on request, never pushed (A §4.3).
7. **Stage** (`--stage decisions.tsv`): closed vocabulary `apply|take-theirs|keep-mine|hold|adapted`, anything else refused before touching anything (lesson A §2.3 #22); pre-update tag; worktree; per-row writes with mode mirroring (`LH:update.sh:726-753`); one commit "LogicLoom update to <sha12>"; hold-back of failing non-safety notes; gate: notes' checks, `settings.json` valid JSON, hooks resolve, protected-tree equality, fail-closed if a safety note is in the plan and the check machinery is missing (`LH:update.sh:1297-1341`). Prints `branch=`, `tag=`, `held=` lines, or `result=aborted unchanged=yes`.
8. **Land** (skill types `git merge --ff-only loom-update/<ts>`): the gate asks; the user approves. Declined → skill runs `--abandon` (worktree, branch, tag removed; nothing changed).
9. **Finish** (`--finish`): records `applied` (tag, commit, upstream sha); post-landing smoke on the live tree (checks again, JSON valid, hooks resolve, no tracked harness file differs from the commit); failure → `git revert` + content verification → `result=reverted` or `reverted-failed`; resolves held/declined records; bumps `.sdd-sync-ref` **inside the update commit** (step 7) so a declined note's paths do not vanish — 1.5(c) carries them.
10. **Report** (skill): three outcomes — Updated / Updated with some held back / Stopped — plus `reverted-failed` worded as "pause, ask for help, tag kept" (`LH:SKILL.md` step 10). Then "run `/git-push` when ready" — the engine never pushes. Suggest a fresh session so new commands load (UNVERIFIED whether Claude Code reloads `.claude/commands/*.md` mid-session, B §7).
11. **Undo** (`--undo`): reverts only the commit recorded in `applied` (`LH:update.sh:2458-2465`), refuses on dirty tree, verifies every touched path matches the tag, refuses a commit that touches the forbidden set; on overlap with later edits, `revert --abort` and name the paths.

Prompt count per update: one (the merge). Plus any `protect-governance-files` ASK only if the skill itself edits a protected path by hand, which the flow never does.

---

## 3. Bootstrap: from 6.x to the new engine (constraint C)

### 3.1 Correction to the brief's premise

The receipt filter the brief cites (`extract-proposals.sh:592-606`) exists only on `dev-main` since `39b59d5` and in no tag or npm version; `v6.6.2:extract-proposals.sh` (428 lines) has no adopted-mode filtering at all (B §0.1; pinned by `test_update_from_6x.sh:62-72`). I read `v6.6.2` lines 70-160 and found only URL resolution, fetch and the sync-ref guard; I did not run it. So an adopted 6.6.x install receives the same unfiltered `sync-ref..upstream` diff a template clone does: every new file is a `clean-add`. Nothing has to "get through" a filter.

### 3.2 The path, both channels

1. Customer runs `/update-framework` (their 6.6.x copy). It fetches, diffs `.sdd-sync-ref..refs/loom-upstream/main`, and offers: `clean-add` for the engine, JSON tools, linter, checks runner, `updates/7.0.0/*`, skill, `update-loom.md` command + bridge stub, the two agents, `retired-plugins.txt`, `update-channels.conf`; `clean-apply` for `plugins/loom-maintenance/commands/update-framework.md` (now a forwarding stub) when pristine, `conflict-review` when customised (they keep the old flow, which still works; `/update-loom` is standalone). The old skill writes with `git show > path` (`v6.6.2:SKILL.md` Step 7) — mode 644, which is why every new script is invoked as `sh <path>`/`bash <path>` and never relies on the exec bit.
2. The old skill tags `loom/pre-update-<ts>` (Step 6), commits, and advances `.sdd-sync-ref` to T (Step 8).
3. **First `/update-loom` run — handover.** `.sdd-sync-ref` = T contains all 7.0.0 notes, so presence alone would say "nothing to do". Rule 1.5(b) runs each note's check against the live tree: retired-plugin wiring, the ladder pattern, the moved docs — whatever fails is offered. Additionally `--detect-base` reports `handover <tag-sha>` when the newest `loom/pre-update-*` tag exists and its tree predates the 7.0.0 notes: paths the customer left as `conflict-review` in the old run (silently dropped, B §0.6) are re-classified against that tag's tree and go through adaptation. Both mechanisms are belt and braces; (b) alone suffices when no tag survives.
4. From then on, only `/update-loom`.

**Adopted channel specifics**: the old path also offers `tests/` and `.github/` changes that are not the adopter's (B §2). Mitigation is release shape, not code: 7.0.0 is a **thin bootstrap release** (engine, notes, agents, skill, command, two config files, forwarding stub) so the proposal list is short and obviously updater-shaped; everything else waits for 7.1.0, which arrives through the new engine with the channel map in force. Root `CLAUDE.md` changes in 7.0.0 are limited to the trim (case 6c) so an adopter sees nothing against their own `CLAUDE.md`.

### 3.3 Who cannot get there through the old path — said plainly

- `.sdd-sync-ref` not an ancestor of public `main` and not in the two-entry remap: v6.3.0/v6.4.1/v6.5.0 tag clones carry `c6092b0` (verified: `git merge-base --is-ancestor c6092b0 origin/main` fails; it is also this dev tree's own stamp). The 6.6.2 extractor exits 3. They must first follow `KNOWN_ISSUES.md:55-112` (manual re-baseline), **or** re-install: template clones re-clone and port their edits; adopted installs run the installer again. Today the installer refuses an already-adopted repo (`preconditions.js:416`). Decision 9.6 proposes `npx logicloom@7 update-bootstrap`, a payload group that installs only the updater set and records a receipt run — the clean fallback, not required for the main path.
- Missing `.sdd-sync-ref`: the old script baselines at tip and adopts nothing (`v6.6.2:extract-proposals.sh:149-155`); a second run then sees only later changes — the engine never arrives through that path. Same fallback.
- Pre-6.2 (`.specify/`) layouts: out of scope, as today (`MIGRATION_GUIDE.md:577-582`).

### 3.4 What `/update-framework` becomes

The plugin command file becomes a ~15-line **forwarding stub**: "This command was renamed `/update-loom`. Run `/update-loom`; if that command is not available in this session, start a new session, or run `sh plugins/loom-maintenance/scripts/update-loom.sh --status` to confirm the engine is present." Not a tombstone (old npm installers print the old name forever, `preconditions.js:416`, `detect.js:625`), not an alias (the bridge manifest maps one name to one source, `.claude/commands/.bridge-manifest.json:27`). The `framework-updater` skill directory is removed; `plugin.json:24,38` lists change; `leak-guard.sh:150-151` KEEP zones move to the new paths.

### 3.5 The test that proves it

`tests/contract/test_update_from_6x.sh` keeps its harness (`archive_v662_tree`, `init_fixture_repo`, `build_sanitized_upstream_tree`, `graft_synthetic_upstream`, the fresh-process extractor runner, `:202-290`) and changes its assertions: (1) both fixtures see every path in a rewritten `tests/fixtures/update-from-6x/v7-machinery-paths.txt` as `clean-add`/`clean-apply`; (2) the forwarding stub is `clean-apply`; (3) after simulated acceptance, the fixture's own just-accepted `update-loom.sh --status` and `--plan` run on the 6.x-shaped tree (the lesson at `test_update_from_6x.sh:~49-56`: run the accepted tool, not this tree's copy); (4) the handover: with `.sdd-sync-ref` at T and a planted retired-plugin wiring defect, `--plan` offers the retired-plugins note via rule 1.5(b). Maintainer-only (skips on a sanitized tree via `tree-provenance.sh`).

---

## 4. Worked cases

### 4.1 Case 6(a): a retired plugin directory, pristine or customised

**Grouping decision**: four notes, one per retirement family, not 23 and not one. One per plugin means up to 23 questions for a customer holding the seven `sdd-domain-*` dirs; one note hides the successor mapping a customer actually needs. Families from `release-migrations.conf:58-84`: `retired-plugins-renamed` (7 `sdd-*` → `loom-*`), `retired-plugins-domain-briefs` (7 → `plugins/loom-governance/domain-briefs/<d>.md`), `retired-plugins-native` (`sdd-dev-loop`, `loom-dev-loop`, `sdd-debug`, `sdd-domain-template`), `retired-components` (`sdd-marketplace` MCP + `.mcp.json` entry, `src/sdd/__init__.py`, `.specify/config/refinement.conf`, `plugins/migrate-to-plugins.sh`, `plugins/create-domain-plugin.sh`). Each note's body carries the family table (name → successor → what to do).

**No ownership inference, and why the engine cannot see these at all**: public `main` was re-rooted at v6.2.0 (`0e28655`), after every `sdd-*` plugin was gone; `git log origin/main -- plugins/sdd-debug` is empty. There is no pristine blob to compare against, and in a 6.6.x customer's base tree the dir is absent too, so to the three-way engine `plugins/sdd-debug/` is an unchanged local-only path — the customer's, as far as git can tell. The design does not guess. The note therefore tests the **outcome** (is anything still routed through it) and offers the deletion as the user's own command:

```
---
id: retired-plugins-native
title: Plugins replaced by Claude Code's own features
purpose: Nothing in this project routes a hook, command, MCP server or bridge stub through sdd-dev-loop, loom-dev-loop, sdd-debug or sdd-domain-template, which were replaced by native /workflow, /loop and /goal and by /create-plugin.
touches:
  - .logic-loom/config/retired-plugins.txt
removes:
  - plugins/sdd-dev-loop
  - plugins/loom-dev-loop
  - plugins/sdd-debug
  - plugins/sdd-domain-template
adds: []
requires: []
safety: false
done-when:
  - "no hook command in .claude/settings.json names a path under any of the four directories"
  - "no .claude/commands/*.md bridge stub points at any of them"
  - ".mcp.json names no server under them"
  - "if any of the four directories is still on disk, the user has been told what replaced it and that it is theirs to keep or remove"
check: retired-plugins-native.check.sh
project-data: false
channels: both
---
## What changed and why
These four plugins were removed in v5.1.0 and v6.2.0 (sdd-debug had no successor; sdd-dev-loop was superseded by native /workflow, /loop and /goal; sdd-domain-template by /create-plugin). A copy may still hold the directory. LogicLoom never deletes it: it may carry your own edits, and nothing in git can tell your work from a stale copy. If nothing is wired to it, keeping it is harmless.
## What a stock file looks like after
.logic-loom/config/retired-plugins.txt lists the four names; nothing else changes unless you remove a directory yourself.
```

Check (POSIX sh, ~60 lines): flatten `settings.json` with `json-flat.sh`; FAIL if any `/hooks/*/*/hooks/*/command` value contains `plugins/sdd-dev-loop/` etc.; FAIL if any `.claude/commands/*.md` contains the path; FAIL if `.mcp.json` (when present) names it; for each directory present on disk print `WARN plugins/sdd-debug is on disk; nothing is wired to it; replaced by: (none). To remove it yourself: git rm -r plugins/sdd-debug` — still exit 0. Last line on FAIL: `hint=Something in your settings or commands still points at a retired plugin; take this update and the pointer is removed, your directory stays.`

Flow: pristine or customised, the directory is **kept**; the user is told once per family, with the exact command, and runs it themselves if they wish (`git rm` is `ask`, `gate-policy.conf:142`; the user typing it is the approval). The wiring, if any, is adapted by `update-adapter` in `settings.json`/stubs under the normal held-row flow. CI stays green through `retired-plugins.txt` feeding the six tolerance consumers (B §0.9). For retirements **after** 7.0.0 the pristine blob exists in public history, so the ordinary classes apply: unchanged → `delete` (taken under the note's yes), changed → `deleted-upstream-kept` (hold → kept unless the user says remove, `LH:update.sh:662-663`). That is "git's own refusal protects edited files" — a blob comparison, not a guess.

### 4.2 Case 6(b): kori-beta's escalation ladder

The file `.logic-loom/memory/amendments.md` is fork-authored (`constitution.md:314`: amendments have "no upstream counterpart"). kori's copy at `:148` carries `- 3 = it changes auth, payment, or customer-data logic` as a fourth line on the same scale as the 0/1/3 reversibility lines (read directly). LogicLoom never shipped that text; the only public ladder doc has no numeric scale (B §0.7). An honest note:

```
---
id: amendments-ladder-plus-three
title: Escalation ladder: the auth/payment line is a +3 on top, not a fourth 3
purpose: A fork's escalation ladder that lists "it changes auth, payment, or customer-data logic" as another 3 on the reversibility scale double-counts nothing and under-scores a reversible auth change; the current form treats it as +3 added on top of whichever reversibility line applies.
touches:
  - .logic-loom/memory/amendments.md
adds: []
requires: []
safety: false
done-when:
  - "no line in amendments.md scores the auth/payment/customer-data case as a bare 3 on the reversibility scale"
  - "the fork's own wording, headings and rationale are otherwise unchanged"
check: amendments-ladder-plus-three.check.sh
project-data: true
channels: both
---
## What this note may claim
Only this: your file matches a known defect pattern. It may not claim LogicLoom shipped the ladder text (it did not), may not cite any maintainer's personal files, and carries the target wording itself:
  Plus, on top of whichever reversibility line applies:
  +3 — it changes auth, payment, or customer-data logic
If your file says it "restates the harness's own operating principles", that sentence is yours; the adapter leaves it unless you ask.
```

Check: the registry's regex (`release-migrations.conf:88`) as a `grep -E` over the file; non-safety, so a match is `WARN` in a customer copy; `hint=Your escalation ladder scores the auth/payment case on the wrong scale; "Adapt my file" fixes that one line and keeps the rest.` The engine has no upstream blob: the row is always held, base = `-`, the adapter is briefed with the note and `tests/fixtures/release-migrations/kori-amendments-ladder.{before,after}` as worked example (keep those fixtures). Declining is final. The adapter output must keep `apps/mobile/...` and every other mandate byte-for-byte; the reviewer's item 8 checks that.

### 4.3 Case 6(c): customised `CLAUDE.md` or `.claude/settings.json`

**`CLAUDE.md`, template channel**: upstream changed it (the trim: −208/+26 lines, B §3.5) and the customer changed it → `merged-clean` or `conflict` (`LH:update.sh:670-671`). Note `claude-md-trim` (non-safety): purpose "CLAUDE.md carries only facts about this repo; the directory tree, the `.brain/` contract and the amendments doctrine live in `.docs/`"; `touches: CLAUDE.md, .docs/architecture/loom-architecture.md, .docs/policies/project-amendments-policy.md, .brain/README.md`; `channels: template` for the `CLAUDE.md` row (an adopter's root `CLAUDE.md` is theirs, `payload-manifest.txt:365`). Check tests the **outcome that can be tested** without judging the customer's prose: the three destination docs exist and carry their headings (`## Directory structure` in `loom-architecture.md`); the adapted `CLAUDE.md` is judged by the reviewer against done-when "the moved sections are not duplicated in CLAUDE.md". Held row → adapter brief says: remove only the sections that moved, keep every customer section and sentence. On `cannot-adapt`: hold; the customer keeps their file and nothing is lost.

**`.claude/settings.json`**: both-changed is always held (`LH:update.sh:666-667`). Template: base = `.sdd-sync-ref:.claude/settings.json`. Adopted: base = `.claude/.logicloom-adopt-settings.json`'s inserted hooks (kori's is present), mine = the adopter's whole file (with their `enabledPlugins`, `permissions.allow`, their `guard-prod-db.sh` hook), theirs = upstream's hooks object. Adapter rules: never drop a base hook, never loosen `permissions`, keep the adopter's own hooks and keys exactly. `--adapt-save` refuses a body that fails upstream's `json-valid.sh`. Gate and smoke: valid JSON; every harness hook command resolves (`bash <relpath>` form); adopter hooks only warned. The write reaches the live tree through the gated merge (one ASK). If a 7.x note ever changes hook wiring it is a protected-path note, so it must ship a check; whether it is `safety: true` is the author's call.

---

## 5. Maintainer side

**How a change becomes a note.** Whoever opens the PR to `dev-main` that touches a shipped path writes or extends a note under `plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/<next-version>/`. The linter (1.8) runs in `plugin-tests.yml` on every push and in `/promote` step 2 (`promote.md:47`). A note touching a protected path, or any `*guard*`/hook script, must ship a check **proven both ways**: `tests/contract/test_update_notes.sh` runs every check against the template tree (must exit 0) and, for safety notes, against a planted-defect fixture listed in the test (must exit non-zero with a `hint=` last line) — `LH:upstream-pr.md:95-108` as a test instead of a prompt.

**Coverage gate** (`update-coverage.sh`, maintainer-only, stripped): at `/promote` step 2 and as a warning on PRs: compute the shipped diff `<last release tag>..dev-main` after applying `template-strip-manifest.txt` and `sanitize-for-template.sh` exclusions (so maintainer-only files and `features/*/` never count — `sanitize-for-template.sh:31-38` deletes them at release); subtract the union of `touches`, `removes` and `adds` across notes in release folders newer than the last tag; any remaining path fails promotion. A deleted `plugins/<x>/` directory with no `removes` row fails the same way — **this replaces `check-release`** (`release-migrations.sh`; the guard ran inside `test_release_migrations.sh:394`, B §4.4). `promote-to-main.yml:139-145` swaps the three retired suites for `test_update_loom.sh`, `test_update_notes.sh`, `test_update_from_6x.sh`; `plugin-tests.yml:308-316` likewise.

**Channel map generation**: `/promote` step 2 regenerates `.logic-loom/config/update-channels.conf` from `payload-manifest.txt` and fails if it is stale (same pattern as `check-generated-freshness.sh`).

**Release shape rule**: 7.0.0 is thin (3.2). The maintainer standing principle (decision 5) becomes a line in `plugins/CONTRIBUTING.md:110-127`'s release checklist: "every adopter-facing change has a note; ask what a 6.x copy and a 7.x copy each see."

---

## 7. Size and risk

### 7.1 Lines

| Component | New | Basis |
|---|---:|---|
| `update-loom.sh` | ~1,900 | 1,800 portable LH lines (A §5.1) − pack machinery + ~250 for channel map, base ladder, `--stage/--finish/--abandon` split, outcome-based applicability, declined records |
| `json-valid.sh` + `json-flat.sh` | 430 | verbatim |
| `updates-lint.sh`, `updates-checks.sh` | ~550 | 243 + 225 ported, plus 1.8 rules and the copy-mode detector |
| Agents (2) | ~150 | |
| Skill + command + stubs | ~320 | |
| Notes + checks for 7.0.0 (8 notes: 4 retired families, ladder, claude-md-trim, engine-arrival, migration-guide-retired) | ~1,100 | 60-90 + 40-70 each |
| `retired-plugins.txt`, `update-channels.conf` | ~80 | |
| `test_update_loom.sh` (engine fixtures), `test_update_notes.sh`, retargeted `test_update_from_6x.sh`, floor additions | ~3,400 | LH `update-cases.sh` case list (A §1.16) minus pack cases |
| `update-coverage.sh` (maintainer) | ~200 | |
| **Total new** | **~8,100** | |
| **Retired** (section 6) | **~18,500** | 4,805 + 790 + 88 + 1,556 + 771 + 364 + 465 + 614 + 5,568 + 2,826 + 121 + 502 |

### 7.2 Risks, ranked

1. **Loose done-when passes an adaptation that reads wrong** (LH §12 #1; applies in full). Silent. Mitigation: reviewer item "is done-when checkable"; plain-words result always shown; protected-path notes need executable checks.
2. **Hooks cannot see inside the engine** (new; B §5.3). Accepted for staging inside `.git/`; landing is a typed, gated merge; no `reset --hard`, no push, ever. Residual: a subagent outside the flow could stage; it cannot land.
3. **Bootstrap populations** (new). Broken-stamp and missing-stamp customers cannot run the old updater; the only remedies are manual repair or re-install (3.3). Unknown how many exist (B §7).
4. **Legacy `sdd-*` dirs are indistinguishable from customer plugins** (new). By design nothing is deleted; the wiring check is the only assertion. A stale dir with no wiring stays forever unless the user removes it.
5. **Adapter drift across many small notes** (LH #2; applies). Per-note approval; adapter sees the current file each time, never a running memory.
6. **Declined non-safety sibling of a safety note** (LH #3). Linter forbids safety→non-safety `requires`; blocked state is explicit.
7. **Maintainer workload** (LH #5; applies more — LogicLoom ships more surface than Launchhouse). Thin 7.0.0; coverage gate warns on PRs and blocks only at promote.
8. **Scope creep into a patch language** (LH #6). Rule: a check that only makes sense against the template's own prose is template-only, never a customer gate.
9. **Stray agent write** (LH #7). Both agents have no write tool and no Bash; deterministic `--adapt-save`.
10. **`settings.json` bridge** (LH #4): LogicLoom has no analogous one-time loss; the restore machinery (`LH:update.sh:1083-1251`) is **not ported** (−170 lines).
11. **Windows/CRLF** (LH #8): the floor is macOS bash 3.2, not Windows; CRLF-insensitive hashing is kept (cheap), the Windows lane is not built.
12. **Worktree in unusual repos** (submodules, LFS, sparse checkout): UNVERIFIED; the `--stage` refusal set is the only guard.

### 7.3 What this model does not guarantee

- It cannot tell a customer-authored file from a stale harness file when no reference blob exists; it keeps it.
- A tampered engine on the customer's disk runs with their rights; mitigation is the skill's bootstrap rule (run upstream's copy when the local one differs, `LH:SKILL.md` step 2) and the notes tree being always-taken; neither is a sandbox.
- Checks are upstream shell executed locally; trust reduces to the pinned upstream URL in `framework-upstream.conf`.
- `declined.tsv`, `held.tsv` and `state/mine/` live in `.git/` and die with a re-clone; the pre-update tag is local unless the user pushes tags.
- The governance hooks still see only typed commands; "nothing irreversible without you" holds because the engine's irreversible-feeling step is typed, not because a hook can prove it.

---

## 8. Build plan

Phases are ordered; work packages inside a phase run concurrently with disjoint files. "Done" is always a green test, not a claim.

**Phase 0 — first slice: prove the model on a fixture** (one worker, high effort)
- WP0.1 owns `plugins/loom-maintenance/scripts/{update-loom.sh,json-valid.sh,json-flat.sh,updates-lint.sh,updates-checks.sh}` and `tests/contract/test_update_loom.sh`. Port the engine with the `--stage/--finish/--abandon` split, outcome-based applicability, `removes`/`project-data`/`channels`, forbidden set, channel map reader. The test builds throwaway upstream+customer repos (the `update-cases.sh` pattern) with two fixture notes (one safety touching a hooks file, one non-safety touching a doc) and drives: plan → ask (simulated) → adapt-save → stage → `git merge --ff-only` → finish → undo; plus hold-back, stale plan, unknown decision, forbidden-tree gate, handover via planted check failure, adopted fixture with channel map.
- Done when: the e2e passes under macOS `/bin/sh` and under `dash` on CI (bash 3.2 floor test gains a "POSIX family executes under sh" section); no skill exists yet.

**Phase 1 — the people-facing layer** (three workers)
- WP1.1 owns `skills/update-loom/SKILL.md`, `commands/update-loom.md`, the forwarding stub for `update-framework.md`, `plugin.json`, bridge sync output.
- WP1.2 owns `.claude/agents/update-adapter.md`, `update-reviewer.md`, and the registry files in section 6's blast radius that name agents.
- WP1.3 owns `tests/contract/test_update_notes.sh` (lint fixtures, proven-both-ways runner).
- Done when: the skill drives Phase 0's fixture by hand once; `test_update_notes.sh` green on the two fixture notes.

**Phase 2 — content and retirement** (three workers)
- WP2.1 owns `plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/7.0.0/*` (8 notes + checks), `.logic-loom/config/retired-plugins.txt`, the kori fixtures' new home.
- WP2.2 owns the six CI tolerance consumers + `validate-plugin-manifests.py` + `constitutional-check.sh` (re-source to the flat list), and the deletions in section 6 (scanner, lib, conf, recorder, old tests, `MIGRATION_GUIDE.md`, `run_all_tests.sh` rows).
- WP2.3 owns the doc/rename sweep (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `START_HERE.md`, `README.md`, `VISION.md`, `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, `FRAMEWORK_SYNC_GUIDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, templates) — `constitution.md` edits are main-agent ASK and listed separately for the user.
- Done when: `run_all_tests.sh` green; `updates-checks.sh` prints `notes=8 ok`; a planted retired-plugin hook in a fixture makes `--plan` offer the family note.

**Phase 3 — release plumbing** (two workers)
- WP3.1 owns `tests/contract/test_update_from_6x.sh` + `v7-machinery-paths.txt` (3.5).
- WP3.2 owns `template-strip-manifest.txt`, `leak-guard.sh:149-153`, `payload-manifest.txt` comments, `.logic-loom/scripts/bash/update-coverage.sh`, channel-map generator, `promote-to-main.yml:139-145`, `plugin-tests.yml:308-316`, `promote.md` step 2.
- Done when: the 6.x test passes on both fixtures; `update-coverage.sh` reports zero uncovered paths for 7.0.0 and fails on a planted uncovered file; `leak-guard` green on a built sanitized tree.

**Phase 4 — optional** `npx logicloom update-bootstrap` (decision 9.6), `closest` base ladder UX, declined-note polish.

Order constraints: 0 → 1 → 2 → 3; 2 and 3 may overlap only on files not shared (they are not). Nothing is committed without the user; `/git-push` at phase ends.

---

## 9. Decisions for the maintainer

1. **Retired-plugin notes: how many?** (a) **Four family notes** (recommended) — one question per family, successor table in the body. (b) One note for all 23 — one question, but the mapping is buried. (c) One note per plugin — exact, but up to 23 questions for one customer.
2. **Where does `git worktree add` run?** (a) **Inside the engine** (recommended) — one prompt per update (the merge); the worktree never leaves `.git/`. (b) Typed by the skill — two more prompts (`worktree add`, `worktree remove`), every mutation visible to the gate.
3. **7.0.0 release shape.** (a) **Thin bootstrap release** (recommended) — updater set only; everything else in 7.1.0 through the new engine. (b) Full release — more value at once, but an adopter's old updater will offer `tests/` and `.github/` changes it cannot explain.
4. **Reviewer agent tools.** (a) **Read/Grep/Glob only** (recommended) — the engine pre-writes the diffs and logs it needs; no agent in the flow can run git. (b) Keep Bash with a prose allowlist as Launchhouse does — more flexible, enforced by nothing.
5. **Declined non-safety notes.** (a) **Re-offer in one line each run** (recommended; Launchhouse's own recommendation). (b) Declined is final — quieter, but a bad-timing decline is permanent. (c) Do not record declines — the Launchhouse behaviour that silently drops them.
6. **Fallback for installs the old updater cannot serve.** (a) **Add `npx logicloom update-bootstrap`** in Phase 4 (recommended) — installs the updater set, records a receipt run, skips the already-adopted refusal. (b) Document the manual repair only (`KNOWN_ISSUES.md`). (c) Tell them to re-clone/re-adopt.
7. **A retired directory the user wants gone.** (a) **The note prints the `git rm -r` command; the user runs it** (recommended) — the gate asks on `git rm`, which is the approval. (b) The engine stages the deletion under the note's yes — fewer steps, but the engine deletes something git cannot prove is stale.
8. **Where notes live.** (a) **`plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/`** (recommended) — plugin-owned data, no exposure to a fork's `.logic-loom` rename. (b) `.logic-loom/updates/` — sits beside `config/`, but a renamed framework dir moves it.
9. **Uninstall record for update-added files.** (a) **Drop the recorder** (recommended) — correctness no longer depends on the receipt; `npx logicloom uninstall` may leave update-added files behind, documented. (b) Keep a trimmed append-only recorder (~150 lines) run by `--finish` in adopted repos.
10. **Engine language rule in the shell policy.** (a) **POSIX sh + awk for the update family, bash 3.2 for the rest** (recommended) — checks run in adopted repos on any machine; CI's `/bin/sh` (dash) becomes a free second interpreter. (b) bash 3.2 for everything — one rule, but the port is rewritten from `sh` and loses the dash check.

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '180,360p' design.md" in /Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration
 succeeded in 0ms:

### 3.5 The test that proves it

`tests/contract/test_update_from_6x.sh` keeps its harness (`archive_v662_tree`, `init_fixture_repo`, `build_sanitized_upstream_tree`, `graft_synthetic_upstream`, the fresh-process extractor runner, `:202-290`) and changes its assertions: (1) both fixtures see every path in a rewritten `tests/fixtures/update-from-6x/v7-machinery-paths.txt` as `clean-add`/`clean-apply`; (2) the forwarding stub is `clean-apply`; (3) after simulated acceptance, the fixture's own just-accepted `update-loom.sh --status` and `--plan` run on the 6.x-shaped tree (the lesson at `test_update_from_6x.sh:~49-56`: run the accepted tool, not this tree's copy); (4) the handover: with `.sdd-sync-ref` at T and a planted retired-plugin wiring defect, `--plan` offers the retired-plugins note via rule 1.5(b). Maintainer-only (skips on a sanitized tree via `tree-provenance.sh`).

## 4. Worked cases

### 4.1 Case 6(a): a retired plugin directory, pristine or customised

**Grouping decision**: four notes, one per retirement family, not 23 and not one. One per plugin means up to 23 questions for a customer holding the seven `sdd-domain-*` dirs; one note hides the successor mapping a customer actually needs. Families from `release-migrations.conf:58-84`: `retired-plugins-renamed` (7 `sdd-*` → `loom-*`), `retired-plugins-domain-briefs` (7 → `plugins/loom-governance/domain-briefs/<d>.md`), `retired-plugins-native` (`sdd-dev-loop`, `loom-dev-loop`, `sdd-debug`, `sdd-domain-template`), `retired-components` (`sdd-marketplace` MCP + `.mcp.json` entry, `src/sdd/__init__.py`, `.specify/config/refinement.conf`, `plugins/migrate-to-plugins.sh`, `plugins/create-domain-plugin.sh`). Each note's body carries the family table (name → successor → what to do).

**No ownership inference, and why the engine cannot see these at all**: public `main` was re-rooted at v6.2.0 (`0e28655`), after every `sdd-*` plugin was gone; `git log origin/main -- plugins/sdd-debug` is empty. There is no pristine blob to compare against, and in a 6.6.x customer's base tree the dir is absent too, so to the three-way engine `plugins/sdd-debug/` is an unchanged local-only path — the customer's, as far as git can tell. The design does not guess. The note therefore tests the **outcome** (is anything still routed through it) and offers the deletion as the user's own command:

```
---
id: retired-plugins-native
title: Plugins replaced by Claude Code's own features
purpose: Nothing in this project routes a hook, command, MCP server or bridge stub through sdd-dev-loop, loom-dev-loop, sdd-debug or sdd-domain-template, which were replaced by native /workflow, /loop and /goal and by /create-plugin.
touches:
  - .logic-loom/config/retired-plugins.txt
removes:
  - plugins/sdd-dev-loop
  - plugins/loom-dev-loop
  - plugins/sdd-debug
  - plugins/sdd-domain-template
adds: []
requires: []
safety: false
done-when:
  - "no hook command in .claude/settings.json names a path under any of the four directories"
  - "no .claude/commands/*.md bridge stub points at any of them"
  - ".mcp.json names no server under them"
  - "if any of the four directories is still on disk, the user has been told what replaced it and that it is theirs to keep or remove"
check: retired-plugins-native.check.sh
project-data: false
channels: both
---
## What changed and why
These four plugins were removed in v5.1.0 and v6.2.0 (sdd-debug had no successor; sdd-dev-loop was superseded by native /workflow, /loop and /goal; sdd-domain-template by /create-plugin). A copy may still hold the directory. LogicLoom never deletes it: it may carry your own edits, and nothing in git can tell your work from a stale copy. If nothing is wired to it, keeping it is harmless.
## What a stock file looks like after
.logic-loom/config/retired-plugins.txt lists the four names; nothing else changes unless you remove a directory yourself.
```

Check (POSIX sh, ~60 lines): flatten `settings.json` with `json-flat.sh`; FAIL if any `/hooks/*/*/hooks/*/command` value contains `plugins/sdd-dev-loop/` etc.; FAIL if any `.claude/commands/*.md` contains the path; FAIL if `.mcp.json` (when present) names it; for each directory present on disk print `WARN plugins/sdd-debug is on disk; nothing is wired to it; replaced by: (none). To remove it yourself: git rm -r plugins/sdd-debug` — still exit 0. Last line on FAIL: `hint=Something in your settings or commands still points at a retired plugin; take this update and the pointer is removed, your directory stays.`

Flow: pristine or customised, the directory is **kept**; the user is told once per family, with the exact command, and runs it themselves if they wish (`git rm` is `ask`, `gate-policy.conf:142`; the user typing it is the approval). The wiring, if any, is adapted by `update-adapter` in `settings.json`/stubs under the normal held-row flow. CI stays green through `retired-plugins.txt` feeding the six tolerance consumers (B §0.9). For retirements **after** 7.0.0 the pristine blob exists in public history, so the ordinary classes apply: unchanged → `delete` (taken under the note's yes), changed → `deleted-upstream-kept` (hold → kept unless the user says remove, `LH:update.sh:662-663`). That is "git's own refusal protects edited files" — a blob comparison, not a guess.

### 4.2 Case 6(b): kori-beta's escalation ladder

The file `.logic-loom/memory/amendments.md` is fork-authored (`constitution.md:314`: amendments have "no upstream counterpart"). kori's copy at `:148` carries `- 3 = it changes auth, payment, or customer-data logic` as a fourth line on the same scale as the 0/1/3 reversibility lines (read directly). LogicLoom never shipped that text; the only public ladder doc has no numeric scale (B §0.7). An honest note:

```
---
id: amendments-ladder-plus-three
title: Escalation ladder: the auth/payment line is a +3 on top, not a fourth 3
purpose: A fork's escalation ladder that lists "it changes auth, payment, or customer-data logic" as another 3 on the reversibility scale double-counts nothing and under-scores a reversible auth change; the current form treats it as +3 added on top of whichever reversibility line applies.
touches:
  - .logic-loom/memory/amendments.md
adds: []
requires: []
safety: false
done-when:
  - "no line in amendments.md scores the auth/payment/customer-data case as a bare 3 on the reversibility scale"
  - "the fork's own wording, headings and rationale are otherwise unchanged"
check: amendments-ladder-plus-three.check.sh
project-data: true
channels: both
---
## What this note may claim
Only this: your file matches a known defect pattern. It may not claim LogicLoom shipped the ladder text (it did not), may not cite any maintainer's personal files, and carries the target wording itself:
  Plus, on top of whichever reversibility line applies:
  +3 — it changes auth, payment, or customer-data logic
If your file says it "restates the harness's own operating principles", that sentence is yours; the adapter leaves it unless you ask.
```

Check: the registry's regex (`release-migrations.conf:88`) as a `grep -E` over the file; non-safety, so a match is `WARN` in a customer copy; `hint=Your escalation ladder scores the auth/payment case on the wrong scale; "Adapt my file" fixes that one line and keeps the rest.` The engine has no upstream blob: the row is always held, base = `-`, the adapter is briefed with the note and `tests/fixtures/release-migrations/kori-amendments-ladder.{before,after}` as worked example (keep those fixtures). Declining is final. The adapter output must keep `apps/mobile/...` and every other mandate byte-for-byte; the reviewer's item 8 checks that.

### 4.3 Case 6(c): customised `CLAUDE.md` or `.claude/settings.json`

**`CLAUDE.md`, template channel**: upstream changed it (the trim: −208/+26 lines, B §3.5) and the customer changed it → `merged-clean` or `conflict` (`LH:update.sh:670-671`). Note `claude-md-trim` (non-safety): purpose "CLAUDE.md carries only facts about this repo; the directory tree, the `.brain/` contract and the amendments doctrine live in `.docs/`"; `touches: CLAUDE.md, .docs/architecture/loom-architecture.md, .docs/policies/project-amendments-policy.md, .brain/README.md`; `channels: template` for the `CLAUDE.md` row (an adopter's root `CLAUDE.md` is theirs, `payload-manifest.txt:365`). Check tests the **outcome that can be tested** without judging the customer's prose: the three destination docs exist and carry their headings (`## Directory structure` in `loom-architecture.md`); the adapted `CLAUDE.md` is judged by the reviewer against done-when "the moved sections are not duplicated in CLAUDE.md". Held row → adapter brief says: remove only the sections that moved, keep every customer section and sentence. On `cannot-adapt`: hold; the customer keeps their file and nothing is lost.

**`.claude/settings.json`**: both-changed is always held (`LH:update.sh:666-667`). Template: base = `.sdd-sync-ref:.claude/settings.json`. Adopted: base = `.claude/.logicloom-adopt-settings.json`'s inserted hooks (kori's is present), mine = the adopter's whole file (with their `enabledPlugins`, `permissions.allow`, their `guard-prod-db.sh` hook), theirs = upstream's hooks object. Adapter rules: never drop a base hook, never loosen `permissions`, keep the adopter's own hooks and keys exactly. `--adapt-save` refuses a body that fails upstream's `json-valid.sh`. Gate and smoke: valid JSON; every harness hook command resolves (`bash <relpath>` form); adopter hooks only warned. The write reaches the live tree through the gated merge (one ASK). If a 7.x note ever changes hook wiring it is a protected-path note, so it must ship a check; whether it is `safety: true` is the author's call.

---

## 5. Maintainer side

**How a change becomes a note.** Whoever opens the PR to `dev-main` that touches a shipped path writes or extends a note under `plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/<next-version>/`. The linter (1.8) runs in `plugin-tests.yml` on every push and in `/promote` step 2 (`promote.md:47`). A note touching a protected path, or any `*guard*`/hook script, must ship a check **proven both ways**: `tests/contract/test_update_notes.sh` runs every check against the template tree (must exit 0) and, for safety notes, against a planted-defect fixture listed in the test (must exit non-zero with a `hint=` last line) — `LH:upstream-pr.md:95-108` as a test instead of a prompt.

**Coverage gate** (`update-coverage.sh`, maintainer-only, stripped): at `/promote` step 2 and as a warning on PRs: compute the shipped diff `<last release tag>..dev-main` after applying `template-strip-manifest.txt` and `sanitize-for-template.sh` exclusions (so maintainer-only files and `features/*/` never count — `sanitize-for-template.sh:31-38` deletes them at release); subtract the union of `touches`, `removes` and `adds` across notes in release folders newer than the last tag; any remaining path fails promotion. A deleted `plugins/<x>/` directory with no `removes` row fails the same way — **this replaces `check-release`** (`release-migrations.sh`; the guard ran inside `test_release_migrations.sh:394`, B §4.4). `promote-to-main.yml:139-145` swaps the three retired suites for `test_update_loom.sh`, `test_update_notes.sh`, `test_update_from_6x.sh`; `plugin-tests.yml:308-316` likewise.

**Channel map generation**: `/promote` step 2 regenerates `.logic-loom/config/update-channels.conf` from `payload-manifest.txt` and fails if it is stale (same pattern as `check-generated-freshness.sh`).

**Release shape rule**: 7.0.0 is thin (3.2). The maintainer standing principle (decision 5) becomes a line in `plugins/CONTRIBUTING.md:110-127`'s release checklist: "every adopter-facing change has a note; ask what a 6.x copy and a 7.x copy each see."

## 7. Size and risk

### 7.1 Lines

| Component | New | Basis |
|---|---:|---|
| `update-loom.sh` | ~1,900 | 1,800 portable LH lines (A §5.1) − pack machinery + ~250 for channel map, base ladder, `--stage/--finish/--abandon` split, outcome-based applicability, declined records |
| `json-valid.sh` + `json-flat.sh` | 430 | verbatim |
| `updates-lint.sh`, `updates-checks.sh` | ~550 | 243 + 225 ported, plus 1.8 rules and the copy-mode detector |
| Agents (2) | ~150 | |
| Skill + command + stubs | ~320 | |
| Notes + checks for 7.0.0 (8 notes: 4 retired families, ladder, claude-md-trim, engine-arrival, migration-guide-retired) | ~1,100 | 60-90 + 40-70 each |
| `retired-plugins.txt`, `update-channels.conf` | ~80 | |
| `test_update_loom.sh` (engine fixtures), `test_update_notes.sh`, retargeted `test_update_from_6x.sh`, floor additions | ~3,400 | LH `update-cases.sh` case list (A §1.16) minus pack cases |
| `update-coverage.sh` (maintainer) | ~200 | |
| **Total new** | **~8,100** | |
| **Retired** (section 6) | **~18,500** | 4,805 + 790 + 88 + 1,556 + 771 + 364 + 465 + 614 + 5,568 + 2,826 + 121 + 502 |

### 7.2 Risks, ranked

1. **Loose done-when passes an adaptation that reads wrong** (LH §12 #1; applies in full). Silent. Mitigation: reviewer item "is done-when checkable"; plain-words result always shown; protected-path notes need executable checks.
2. **Hooks cannot see inside the engine** (new; B §5.3). Accepted for staging inside `.git/`; landing is a typed, gated merge; no `reset --hard`, no push, ever. Residual: a subagent outside the flow could stage; it cannot land.
3. **Bootstrap populations** (new). Broken-stamp and missing-stamp customers cannot run the old updater; the only remedies are manual repair or re-install (3.3). Unknown how many exist (B §7).
4. **Legacy `sdd-*` dirs are indistinguishable from customer plugins** (new). By design nothing is deleted; the wiring check is the only assertion. A stale dir with no wiring stays forever unless the user removes it.
5. **Adapter drift across many small notes** (LH #2; applies). Per-note approval; adapter sees the current file each time, never a running memory.
6. **Declined non-safety sibling of a safety note** (LH #3). Linter forbids safety→non-safety `requires`; blocked state is explicit.
7. **Maintainer workload** (LH #5; applies more — LogicLoom ships more surface than Launchhouse). Thin 7.0.0; coverage gate warns on PRs and blocks only at promote.
8. **Scope creep into a patch language** (LH #6). Rule: a check that only makes sense against the template's own prose is template-only, never a customer gate.
9. **Stray agent write** (LH #7). Both agents have no write tool and no Bash; deterministic `--adapt-save`.
10. **`settings.json` bridge** (LH #4): LogicLoom has no analogous one-time loss; the restore machinery (`LH:update.sh:1083-1251`) is **not ported** (−170 lines).
11. **Windows/CRLF** (LH #8): the floor is macOS bash 3.2, not Windows; CRLF-insensitive hashing is kept (cheap), the Windows lane is not built.
12. **Worktree in unusual repos** (submodules, LFS, sparse checkout): UNVERIFIED; the `--stage` refusal set is the only guard.

### 7.3 What this model does not guarantee

- It cannot tell a customer-authored file from a stale harness file when no reference blob exists; it keeps it.
- A tampered engine on the customer's disk runs with their rights; mitigation is the skill's bootstrap rule (run upstream's copy when the local one differs, `LH:SKILL.md` step 2) and the notes tree being always-taken; neither is a sandbox.
- Checks are upstream shell executed locally; trust reduces to the pinned upstream URL in `framework-upstream.conf`.
- `declined.tsv`, `held.tsv` and `state/mine/` live in `.git/` and die with a re-clone; the pre-update tag is local unless the user pushes tags.
- The governance hooks still see only typed commands; "nothing irreversible without you" holds because the engine's irreversible-feeling step is typed, not because a hook can prove it.

---

## 8. Build plan

Phases are ordered; work packages inside a phase run concurrently with disjoint files. "Done" is always a green test, not a claim.

**Phase 0 — first slice: prove the model on a fixture** (one worker, high effort)
- WP0.1 owns `plugins/loom-maintenance/scripts/{update-loom.sh,json-valid.sh,json-flat.sh,updates-lint.sh,updates-checks.sh}` and `tests/contract/test_update_loom.sh`. Port the engine with the `--stage/--finish/--abandon` split, outcome-based applicability, `removes`/`project-data`/`channels`, forbidden set, channel map reader. The test builds throwaway upstream+customer repos (the `update-cases.sh` pattern) with two fixture notes (one safety touching a hooks file, one non-safety touching a doc) and drives: plan → ask (simulated) → adapt-save → stage → `git merge --ff-only` → finish → undo; plus hold-back, stale plan, unknown decision, forbidden-tree gate, handover via planted check failure, adopted fixture with channel map.
- Done when: the e2e passes under macOS `/bin/sh` and under `dash` on CI (bash 3.2 floor test gains a "POSIX family executes under sh" section); no skill exists yet.

**Phase 1 — the people-facing layer** (three workers)
- WP1.1 owns `skills/update-loom/SKILL.md`, `commands/update-loom.md`, the forwarding stub for `update-framework.md`, `plugin.json`, bridge sync output.
- WP1.2 owns `.claude/agents/update-adapter.md`, `update-reviewer.md`, and the registry files in section 6's blast radius that name agents.
- WP1.3 owns `tests/contract/test_update_notes.sh` (lint fixtures, proven-both-ways runner).

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '95,215p' design.md" in /Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration
 succeeded in 0ms:

Any path under `plugins/loom-maintenance/updates/` that differs from upstream is `take`/`delete`, never held or kept (`LH:update.sh:560-576`), and is exempt from `.gitignore` suppression (`LH:update.sh:381-386`). A customer cannot edit a safety note away.

### 1.7 Check contract

Runs as `sh <file>` with the repo root as cwd and `REPO_ROOT` exported; POSIX sh + awk only; exit 0 = purpose holds; on failure the **last** line is `hint=<one plain sentence>` (`LH:README.md`, The check file). JSON is read by content via `json-flat.sh`, never by line shape (lesson A §2.2 #8). Hook commands in LogicLoom's `settings.json` are `bash <relpath>` (`.claude/settings.json:2-89`; kori-beta identical, plus one adopter hook `bash scripts/hooks/guard-prod-db.sh`), so the shipped `hooks-resolve` helper extracts the first `*.sh` token, requires it to exist, be readable and pass `sh -n`/`bash -n`, and **never executes it** (`LH:update.sh:1487-1524`). It gates only on paths under harness-owned trees (`.claude/hooks/`, `plugins/loom-*/`, `.logic-loom/`) and warns on the rest — a missing adopter hook is theirs, not a reason to stop their update (template-conformance rule, A §2.2 #7).

Two check kinds, by note: **safety** — must pass in every copy; missing check, `check: none`, malformed `safety:` or any non-zero exit is FAIL (`LH:updates-checks.sh:165-214`). **Non-safety** — in a customer copy a failure is `WARN` and the run stays green; in the template repo it is FAIL (`LH:updates-checks.sh:93-105, 204-206`). Copy mode detector: template = `tests/lib/tree-provenance.sh` says "maintainer" (its five marker files all present, `:28-40`); anything else is a customer copy. The hold-back pass (`LH:update.sh:1855-2072`) re-runs each non-safety check in the worktree and sets aside only the failing improvement.

### 1.8 Linter

Port of `LH:updates-lint.sh:155-236` plus: `removes` absent upstream; `project-data` allowlist; protected-path ⇒ `check` required; `channels` ∈ {both, template, adopted}; forbidden-touch set; no `ID_MARKERS` (`leak-guard.sh` list: `brian@kelleysd.com`, `kelleysd.com/`, `ioun-ai`, `/workspaces/`) and no `~/.claude` reference in any note body (B §0.7). Runs in `plugin-tests.yml`, in `/promote` step 2, and first inside `updates-checks.sh`.

### 1.9 `requires` chains, declined notes

`requires` is schema-only in Launchhouse (A §1.13). Here: `--plan` orders notes topologically; a note whose `requires` names a declined or held note is shown as **blocked** with the blocker's title and is not applied; a safety note may not `requires` a non-safety note (linter) so the half-applied case in LH risk 3 cannot arise. Declined: 1.5.

---

## 2. The `/update-loom` flow

Actors: **engine** (`update-loom.sh`, deterministic, POSIX sh); **skill** (main agent, the only thing that talks to the user and the only thing that types git); **update-adapter** and **update-reviewer** (project agents, `tools: Read, Grep, Glob`, no Bash — the engine pre-writes every diff and log they need into state, so the reviewer's `git show/diff/log` allowance in `LH:update-reviewer.md:18-20` is unnecessary and its prose-only enforcement is gone); **user**.

### 2.1 Git operations mapped to `gate-policy.conf` (constraint A)

The hooks are a string gate on the command the agent types (`git-safety-gate.sh:12-16`); git run inside `sh update-loom.sh` is invisible to them (B §5.3). The design therefore puts every git mutation into one of two buckets:

| Step | Who runs it | Where it happens | Gate verdict | Why acceptable |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `git fetch <url> +refs/heads/main:refs/loom-upstream/main` | engine (`--plan`) | local ref only | would be `git.fetch = silent` (`gate-policy.conf:208`) | same as today's extractor |
| `git tag loom/pre-update-<ts>` | engine (`--stage`) | local tag | `git.tag.write = silent` (`:178`) | the old skill does the same (`v6.6.2:SKILL.md` Step 6) |
| `git worktree add -b loom-update/<ts> <state>/wt HEAD`, `git add`, `git commit`, `--amend` (hold-back), `worktree remove`, `branch -D` | engine (`--stage`, `--abandon`, `--finish`) | inside `.git/loom/update/`; live tree untouched | `worktree.write`/`branch.write` are `ask` (`:151,156`) **when typed**; here invisible | reversibility 0: nothing in the working tree changes; the worktree and branch are throwaway; the refusal set (`is_clean`, HEAD and upstream unchanged since plan, `LH:update.sh:1631-1653`) runs first. Decision 9.2 offers the visible alternative |
| **`git merge --ff-only loom-update/<ts>`** | **skill, typed visibly** | live branch | **`git.merge = ask` (`:103`)** | the one prompt per update; it is the moment the live tree changes, it carries a commit touching protected paths, and it is where Principle VI's approval belongs. The command text names no protected token, so `protect-governance-files.sh`'s Bash branch stays quiet; one prompt, not two |
| post-landing smoke, auto-undo via `git revert --no-edit` | engine (`--finish`) | live branch | `git.revert = silent` (`:196`) | additive by construction; verified by content (`LH:update.sh:2439-2448`) |
| `--undo` → `git revert --no-edit <recorded commit>` | engine, after the user says "undo" | live branch | silent | same |
| `git reset --hard` | **never** | — | `git.reset.hard = ask` (`:120`) | Launchhouse's undo-verification failure path (`LH:update.sh:2522`) is replaced: `git revert --abort` where a revert is in progress, otherwise print `result=reverted-failed`, `pre_update_tag=` and stop; a person decides |
| `git push` | **never** | — | FLOOR | Launchhouse's skill pushes (`LH:SKILL.md` step 10.1); LogicLoom does not. `/git-push` is the user's path |

Subagents: neither agent has Bash, so no agent in this flow can run the engine or git. Landing requires a typed git command, which `subagent-git-guard.sh` denies for any subagent in every posture (`gate-policy.conf:56-58`). A subagent outside this flow could at most run `--stage` (staging a worktree inside `.git`), never land it. UNVERIFIED: no environment marker tells a child process it was launched by a subagent (B §5.3), so the engine itself cannot refuse on that basis.

Protected-path writes (`.claude/settings.json`, hooks): the engine writes them only in the worktree; they reach the live tree through the gated merge. The adapter never writes anything (constraint H); the skill saves its returned body with `--adapt-save`, which validates path membership, forbidden set, symlinks, and JSON validity with upstream's own validator saved at plan time (`LH:update.sh:968-1081`, `:871-876`).

### 2.2 Step by step (both channels; channel differences in brackets)

0. **Preflight** (skill → engine `--status`): channel, `clean` (ignores untracked files outside harness paths — kori has an untracked `.claude/launch.json`, B §2), `in_progress`, upstream URL from `framework-upstream.conf`. **Bootstrap check** (section 3): if the local engine differs from upstream's copy, run upstream's copy from `<gitdir>/loom/bootstrap/` for this one update (`LH:SKILL.md` "First update on an older copy"). Dirty tree → stop and ask the user to commit; the skill does not commit for them.
1. **Base** (`--detect-base`): `recorded <sha>` from `.sdd-sync-ref` if it is a commit reachable from `refs/loom-upstream/main`; else the known-bad remap (`v6.6.2:extract-proposals.sh:127-138`, kept); else `closest <sha> <n>` by fewest differing harness-owned files across first-parent public history (`LH:update.sh:300-330`), confirmed by the user; else `none` → stop with the repair text from `KNOWN_ISSUES.md:55-112`. `--set-base` commits `.sdd-sync-ref` alone (lesson A §2.2 #15; `git.commit = silent`).
2. **Plan** (`--plan`, 10-minute Bash timeout, idempotent): fetch; copy notes+checks from upstream into state; classify every path; apply channel map; compute note applicability (1.5); write `plan.tsv`, `notes.tsv` (with a `blocked`/`declined-before` column), `adapt.tsv` with base/theirs/mine sides, `diffs/`, and `reviewer/<path>.log` (pre-materialised `git log` for the reviewer). If every row is `keep`/unchanged and no note applies: "You are up to date."
3. **Ask per improvement** (skill, AskUserQuestion, batched): for **every** applying note, not only those with held rows (fixes A §3.1). Safety: *Apply* / *Tell me more*. Non-safety: *Take this update* / *Keep mine as it is*. Declined-before: one line, *Still skip* recommended. `project-data`: *Adapt my file* / *Leave it*. Blocked: shown, not asked. Paths no note covers (the bootstrap span only, section 5 keeps it empty afterwards): one group, "other upstream changes", with the reviewer's bullets.
4. **Adapt** (skill dispatches `update-adapter` per held row whose note was approved, concurrently): brief = path, the three sides from state, the approved notes' purpose/done-when/body (`LH:update-adapter.md` rules carry over: never drop a base hook, never loosen a permission, safety fix wins over the customisation that is the hole, preserve shape and CRLF, JSON must parse). Skill writes the body to `state/adapt/out/<path>` and calls `--adapt-save`. `cannot-adapt`/refusal: safety → `take-theirs` (customer's copy preserved at the tag and in `state/mine/`); otherwise `hold`.
5. **Second read** (`update-reviewer`, read-only): waterfall items 1, 3, 7, 8 of `LH:update-reviewer.md` (dependency grep, settings customisation, merged-clean sense read, adapted-vs-purpose) plus the new item from LH §11 Q3: *is each prose done-when checkable and does it match the purpose*. May only make a row more cautious.
6. **Show and one yes** (skill): what is new; kept as you made it; one sentence per adapted file; held items; blocked items; then one clear yes. Developer audience: paths and a diff are offered on request, never pushed (A §4.3).
7. **Stage** (`--stage decisions.tsv`): closed vocabulary `apply|take-theirs|keep-mine|hold|adapted`, anything else refused before touching anything (lesson A §2.3 #22); pre-update tag; worktree; per-row writes with mode mirroring (`LH:update.sh:726-753`); one commit "LogicLoom update to <sha12>"; hold-back of failing non-safety notes; gate: notes' checks, `settings.json` valid JSON, hooks resolve, protected-tree equality, fail-closed if a safety note is in the plan and the check machinery is missing (`LH:update.sh:1297-1341`). Prints `branch=`, `tag=`, `held=` lines, or `result=aborted unchanged=yes`.
8. **Land** (skill types `git merge --ff-only loom-update/<ts>`): the gate asks; the user approves. Declined → skill runs `--abandon` (worktree, branch, tag removed; nothing changed).
9. **Finish** (`--finish`): records `applied` (tag, commit, upstream sha); post-landing smoke on the live tree (checks again, JSON valid, hooks resolve, no tracked harness file differs from the commit); failure → `git revert` + content verification → `result=reverted` or `reverted-failed`; resolves held/declined records; bumps `.sdd-sync-ref` **inside the update commit** (step 7) so a declined note's paths do not vanish — 1.5(c) carries them.
10. **Report** (skill): three outcomes — Updated / Updated with some held back / Stopped — plus `reverted-failed` worded as "pause, ask for help, tag kept" (`LH:SKILL.md` step 10). Then "run `/git-push` when ready" — the engine never pushes. Suggest a fresh session so new commands load (UNVERIFIED whether Claude Code reloads `.claude/commands/*.md` mid-session, B §7).
11. **Undo** (`--undo`): reverts only the commit recorded in `applied` (`LH:update.sh:2458-2465`), refuses on dirty tree, verifies every touched path matches the tag, refuses a commit that touches the forbidden set; on overlap with later edits, `revert --abort` and name the paths.

Prompt count per update: one (the merge). Plus any `protect-governance-files` ASK only if the skill itself edits a protected path by hand, which the flow never does.

---

## 3. Bootstrap: from 6.x to the new engine (constraint C)

### 3.1 Correction to the brief's premise

The receipt filter the brief cites (`extract-proposals.sh:592-606`) exists only on `dev-main` since `39b59d5` and in no tag or npm version; `v6.6.2:extract-proposals.sh` (428 lines) has no adopted-mode filtering at all (B §0.1; pinned by `test_update_from_6x.sh:62-72`). I read `v6.6.2` lines 70-160 and found only URL resolution, fetch and the sync-ref guard; I did not run it. So an adopted 6.6.x install receives the same unfiltered `sync-ref..upstream` diff a template clone does: every new file is a `clean-add`. Nothing has to "get through" a filter.

### 3.2 The path, both channels

1. Customer runs `/update-framework` (their 6.6.x copy). It fetches, diffs `.sdd-sync-ref..refs/loom-upstream/main`, and offers: `clean-add` for the engine, JSON tools, linter, checks runner, `updates/7.0.0/*`, skill, `update-loom.md` command + bridge stub, the two agents, `retired-plugins.txt`, `update-channels.conf`; `clean-apply` for `plugins/loom-maintenance/commands/update-framework.md` (now a forwarding stub) when pristine, `conflict-review` when customised (they keep the old flow, which still works; `/update-loom` is standalone).
