codex
The design is promising, but it is not safe to implement as written. The largest problems are structural: “improvements” are still applied as whole-file snapshots, bootstrap is non-atomic, security preservation is mostly an agent instruction, and undo depends on ephemeral local state.

## Release-blocking findings

1. **Per-improvement approval is not actually enforceable.** The user approves notes, but staging ultimately applies one decision per path and may take the entire upstream file. If two notes touch one file and the user accepts one but declines the other, a clean `take` applies both changes. The same problem exists for unrelated changes hidden in a covered file. This is the file-vs-note mismatch identified in [Launchhouse study §3.4](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-launchhouse.md:243>) but not solved by [Design §2.2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:137>).

   Require either non-overlapping notes, composite approval for every overlapping group, or per-note patches/commits. Before landing, verify that the staged diff contains exactly the changes authorized by those decisions—not merely paths listed in `touches`.

2. **The 6.x bootstrap is not atomic.** The old updater presents the engine, skill, agents, commands, checks, notes, configs, manifests, and forwarding stub as independent file proposals. A user can accept only some of them. The forwarding stub can then disable the working updater while `/update-loom` is incomplete. The proposed test accepts every machinery path and therefore misses this failure. See [Design §3.2–3.5](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:162>) and [LogicLoom study §2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:97>).

   Bootstrap needs one self-contained artifact or a manifest-hash completeness check. Do not replace `/update-framework` with the forwarding stub until the new engine proves that its complete bundle is present and runnable.

3. **The engine cannot reach all supported old populations.** Broken-stamp installs and missing-`.sdd-sync-ref` installs cannot receive it through their existing updater. The only clean fallback, `npx logicloom update-bootstrap`, is optional Phase 4, while the current installer refuses already-adopted repositories. That means the answer to “can it actually arrive in both channels?” is “only for healthy 6.6.x installs.” See [Design §3.3](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:171>) and [LogicLoom study §0.5](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:11>).

   The bootstrap command must be part of the initial release and support both existing template clones and already-adopted npm installations. It is a release prerequisite, not optional polish.

4. **The “thin bootstrap” release is internally inconsistent.** The design also puts the `CLAUDE.md` trim and other content changes into 7.0.0. The old adopted updater is unfiltered and will offer an adopter’s root `CLAUDE.md`, tests, CI, and other template-only paths. Saying the trim is the only root change does not make it invisible; it becomes `conflict-review`. Compare [Design §3.2 and §4.3](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:162>) with [LogicLoom study §2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:97>).

   The bridge release should contain only the atomic updater bundle and compatibility stub. Defer all ordinary improvements, including the `CLAUDE.md` trim, until the new channel map is active.

5. **A failed safety adaptation silently becomes wholesale replacement.** [Design §2.2 step 4](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:137>) says `cannot-adapt` on a safety note becomes `take-theirs`. The earlier “apply this safety improvement” approval is not informed consent to discard every customization in that file. This directly contradicts the §0 promise that nothing is overwritten without the user’s yes.

   If a safety adaptation fails, abort that improvement—or the entire update. Replacing the file should require a new, explicit confirmation showing exactly which custom hunks will be lost. “Safety cannot be skipped” should mean “the update cannot proceed,” not “the engine may overwrite.”

6. **The design knowingly bypasses LogicLoom’s git governance.** Worktree creation, commits, amendments, branch deletion, tag changes, base-setting commits, and automatic reverts occur inside the engine where hooks cannot inspect them. Only the final merge is visible. That violates the stronger Principle VI requirement that every git mutation receive specific approval, even if most mutations are locally reversible. See [Design §2.1](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:118>) and [LogicLoom study §5.3](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:283>).

   Either issue the governed operations visibly or obtain explicit approval for a precisely enumerated mutation transaction. Hidden automatic `git revert`, `branch -D`, and tag deletion should not be treated as “silent” simply because policy currently classifies them that way.

7. **Security preservation is prose, not an invariant.** The adapter is told not to drop hooks or loosen permissions, but `--adapt-save` proves only membership and JSON validity. The hook gate proves only that a referenced script exists and parses. It does not detect:

   - Removed or weakened deny rules.
   - Broadened allow rules.
   - Changed hook matchers or ordering.
   - A command redirected to a harmless existing script.
   - Removal of adopter-owned hooks.
   - Changes to `.claude/policies/tool-restrictions.json`, which the current protected-path floor does not cover.

   Worse, protected-path notes need a check but `safety` remains “the author’s call,” while `project-data` notes—including `gate-policy.conf` posture lines—are forbidden from being safety notes. See [Design §1.3 and §4.3](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:61>) and [LogicLoom study §5.4–5.6](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:292>).

   Define a complete security-surface list and require deterministic before/after invariants. Any intentional relaxation should be a separate, exact-diff approval—not something an adaptation may infer.

8. **Checks execute arbitrary upstream shell with the user’s rights.** They run during planning, staging, and after landing. A buggy or compromised check can modify product files, secrets, refs, or external state before the user approves the improvement. The product tree hash does not prove that checks were side-effect-free, especially for untracked files and live-tree execution. Trust also depends on an upstream URL whose pinning and override policy are not fully specified. See [Design §1.7 and §7.3](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:98>) and [Launchhouse study §3.12](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-launchhouse.md:243>).

   Checks need a read-only sandbox or strict before/after filesystem and git-state verification, with network disabled. Applicability checks should never run directly against the live repository. The upstream identity should be hard-pinned or cryptographically verified.

## Concrete logic bugs

9. **Two bootstrap notes will not be offered as written.**

   - `project-data` notes are exempt from check-based applicability and declined-state applicability. After the old updater advances `.sdd-sync-ref` to the release containing the note, the ladder note fails all three applicability conditions and is never offered.
   - The retired-plugin check exits 0 when an unwired retired directory remains, merely printing a warning. After bootstrap, the note is therefore not applicable, so the user is never shown the promised replacement/removal explanation.

   The handover tag does not fix either local-only path. See [Design §1.5](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:86>), [§3.2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:162>), [§4.1](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:189>), and [§4.2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:229>).

   Separate “should be offered” predicates from “outcome is satisfied” checks, and record acknowledgement independently.

10. **Checks can still pass vacuously.** Important examples:

   - `claude-md-trim` checks only that destination headings exist; removal of duplicated content is left to reviewer judgment.
   - A protected non-safety note is not clearly required to have a failing negative fixture.
   - Tests prove checks against the framework tree, not both customer channels or partial adopted payloads.
   - Hook resolution proves syntax, not actual enforcement.
   - A broad note can test one bullet while silently missing another.
   - Non-safety failures remain warnings in customer copies.

   See [Design §1.7, §4.3, and §5](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:98>) and the inherited warning in [Launchhouse study §3.5–3.8](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-launchhouse.md:243>).

   Every executable check should have positive and negative fixtures for every applicable channel. The runner should also fail checks that execute no assertions or inspect none of their declared targets.

11. **Adopted ownership is modeled too coarsely.** A current generated channel map cannot tell which optional payload groups a particular adopter installed, which writes failed, or how an older manifest classified a path. A missing or corrupt receipt is currently treated as a template clone, which is the dangerous direction: customer root files may become framework-owned. See [Design §1.4](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:75>) and [LogicLoom study §1.4 and §6.1](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:79>).

   Use the actual receipt/install profile plus a versioned channel map. If channel ownership is ambiguous, fail closed and skip the path.

12. **The channel schema lacks the granularity the worked case requires.** `channels` is defined per note, but §4.3 says `CLAUDE.md` should be template-only while other paths in the same note may apply to both channels. Checks also need to understand the effective per-channel subset. As written, that cannot be represented cleanly.

   Make channel/address metadata per touched row, or split the improvement into channel-specific notes with channel-specific checks.

13. **Symlink and metadata safety is incomplete.** Symlinks are rejected only by `--adapt-save`; clean `take`, add, and delete writes are not specified to reject symlinks or symlinked parent components. A redirect into a checked-out symlink can modify something outside the worktree. The inherited classifier also ignores mode-only changes, a known Launchhouse defect. See [Design §2.1–2.2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:118>) and [Launchhouse study §3.9](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-launchhouse.md:243>).

   Resolve every parent with `lstat`, refuse traversal, write through temporary regular files plus atomic rename, and compare full git entry metadata—not only normalized bytes.

## Undo and interaction

14. **Undo is only one-deep and clone-local.** `--undo` relies on the single `state/applied` record and a local tag. Re-cloning loses the state and tag; another update overwrites the active record; rebasing or squashing can make the tool refuse. The design also does not say how undo restores `held.tsv`/`declined.tsv` bookkeeping or verifies modes and symlink types. See [Design §2.2 step 11 and §7.3](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:137>).

   Put a machine-readable update manifest in each update commit, support `--undo <update-id>`, verify exact tree entries and the absence of extra paths, and transactionally reverse updater state. Keep automatic recovery separate from user-requested undo.

15. **The prompt-count claim hides approval fatigue.** A run has one question per applicable note, repeated “still skip?” questions, a final confirmation, and the governance merge prompt. Safety notes offer only “Apply” or “Tell me more.” That is not “one prompt per update” in any user-relevant sense. See [Design §2.2 and decision 9.5](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:137>).

   Use one digest with per-item toggles, isolate security-sensitive items, and persist decisions by note-content digest. A decline should be snoozed until the note changes or the user explicitly revisits it, not re-offered on every run.

16. **At least one worked example depends on files absent from npm-adopted repositories.** The ladder adapter is to be briefed with `tests/fixtures/...`, but adopted payloads do not contain `tests/`. More generally, worktrees lack ignored dependencies and customer environment files. See [Design §4.2](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/design.md:229>) and [LogicLoom study §5.6 and §6.1](</Users/bkelley/kelleysd-apps/LogicLoom/features/update-loom/exploration/study-logicloom.md:308>).

   Embed required examples in the shipped note/state, and test the real sanitized template and npm payloads—not only the framework repository.

The minimum safe redesign is: an atomic, universally available bootstrap; note-level patches or enforced overlap groups; deterministic security non-regression checks; sandboxed checks; exact staged-diff allowlisting; and durable, multi-update undo metadata. No files were modified.
tokens used
153,656
The design is promising, but it is not safe to implement as written. The largest problems are structural: “improvements” are still applied as whole-file snapshots, bootstrap is non-atomic, security preservation is mostly an agent instruction, and undo depends on ephemeral local state.
